# LORE_MAPPING — Slice 1: Qing Mao Mountain and the Southern Border

**Status: Phase 1 approved (2026-10-13). The rank 5–9 part (MOD_SPEC_2 Phase B) is at the end, drafted 2026-10-18.** Round 2 checked the user's lore answers against the novel's raw text;
round 3 applied the user's decisions (the Wu clan as fourth major, a hunger meter, spirit springs as the heart of the
map); round 4 made hunger deaths a per-Gu roll (option B) and set the tuning rule: autoplay with Strategic Balance
decides, no manual tuning first. Phase 2 builds Slice 1 from this plan.

One row per planned object (125 rows): English name (fan-translation term), Chinese original, one-line lore
basis, mechanical role, the exact uniques, and canon status.

* **CANON**: taken directly from the novel. **ADAPTED**: a novel element bent to fit a 4X game. **INVENTED**:
  mechanical glue with no novel source.
* **[VERIFY]**: canon is uncertain; the note says what. A *Note* on a row without [VERIFY] records what the research
  confirmed or what is the user's own design choice.
* The Chinese column shows each term once, on its primary object, and stays empty for invented objects. Chinese goes
  into civilopedia text only; object names are ASCII.
* Lore bases are my own one-line paraphrases. Mechanical effects are 4X mappings, never canon claims.
* "Stones" means the Gold stat (primeval stones). "Upkeep" means unit maintenance. P1–P5 are the pillars of
  MOD_SPEC §0.

**Sources.** Round 2 rests on a fact check of 11 claims against the raw novel text (two independent mirrors for 9
of them; one mirror plus an English translation or wiki for the other two), each re-checked by an independent
skeptic; all 11 verdicts are high-confidence. A final lore review of this document added fan-translation checks. Facts that check did not cover keep
[VERIFY].

**How the uniques were checked.** Since Phase 2 every unique in this document is in the mod itself: `jsons/`,
generated by `tools/gen_ruleset.py` from `data/*.csv` (during Phase 1 the same content came from a throwaway
prototype). The mod passes `tools/check_all` (schema 0/0, uniques 0/0, references 0/0, packaging OK) and **Unciv
4.22.8's own validator (`mod-ci`) with 0 errors and 0 warnings**. `tools/ladder_check.py` walks all 14,742 reachable
unit states of the refinement ladder and finds 0 errors. A self-test fails if this document and `jsons/` disagree, if
`jsons/` is stale against `data/`, or if the ladder check fails. To re-run: `tools/check_all`.

Engine behaviour the docs do not state is marked **[src]** (read from the 4.22.8 source). It must be confirmed in
game before content depends on it (see [Engine facts this plan relies on](#engine-facts-this-plan-relies-on)).

### Hunger

A literal per-Gu hunger meter (a number that drains every turn) cannot be stored in Unciv's ruleset: units have no
counters. The engine does offer **timed statuses** and a trigger when a status runs out, which gives the same
behaviour [src `UnitTurnManager.startTurn`, `MapUnit.removeStatus`]:

1. **Fed:** the Gu works normally.
2. **Weakened:** as soon as its food is in deficit, the Gu's effects switch off (each effect carries
   `<when above [amount] [stat/resource]>` on its food), and the engine adds its own −25% for a missing resource.
3. **Hungry:** at the first turn start in deficit the unit gets a hunger status for that food (Orchid, Pork, Jade or
   Wine Hunger): 3 turns for a rank-1 Gu, 5 for a rank-2 Gu.
4. **Dying:** if the food is still short when the status runs out (after 2 more turns for rank 1, 4 for rank 2), the
   Gu dies with a 50% chance; a survivor gets the status again at the next turn start and rolls again 3 (rank 1) or 5
   (rank 2) turns later. Under a lasting deficit a rank-1 Gu lives 5 turns on average, a rank-2 Gu 9. Rank-1 Gu eat
   often in canon (a Moonlight Gu twice a day), rank-2 Gu rarely (White Jade Gu every 20 days), so the higher rank
   holds out longer.
5. **Recovered:** once the food is back, the status removes itself and the Gu works again.

**Why a roll and not a fixed death (round 4, your option B).** Gaining or losing a Gu does not refresh the civ's
resource count [src], so a fixed death would kill every holder of that food at the same turn start, even when one
death would close the gap. The roll is seeded per unit (its name, tile and movement) and per turn [src
`Conditionals.getStateBasedRandom`, `GameContext.hashCode`], so holders die one by one and the deficit can close
before all of them are gone. **Tuning rule (yours):** the autoplay's rank-1 hunger death rate decides whether the
rank-1 threshold rises from 2 turns to 3 or 4; no manual tuning before that data exists.

A killer move whose rank-2 Gu died goes at the next turn start, and the unit can climb the ladder again.

## Path choice: why two paths, and which

**Spec tension.** §6 S5 suggests about six paths for Slice 1; §8 allows two path branches. §8 is the hard budget, so
Slice 1 ships two and the rest become the roadmap.

**Chosen: Moon Path (月道, your design choice) and Strength Path (力道, canon), mutually exclusive.**

1. The anchors. Moonlight Gu fed on moon-orchid petals is the Gu Yue emblem (canon); the novel gives it no path, so
   the moon path is an adaptation you chose. The strength path is canon (the Wu clan's leader walks it), and the boar
   Gu are early beast-strength Gu on it (one branch, not its archetype). The Xiong emblem is the Bear Strength Gu.
2. Two combat styles (round 5c): Strength hits hardest in melee; Moon is a lighter melee skirmisher that throws a moon
   blade before every clash with a unit (40% of its strength, `Before engaging in combat performs an extra ranged attack
   with [amount]% of melee combat strength`). Both can take cities; a ranged Moon could not, so Moon clans never won a
   war in autoplay (docs/AUTOPLAY.md, round 5c).
3. Opposite pressure on the economy: Moon costs fewer stones (+75/150/225% upkeep) and eats Moon Orchid; Strength
   costs more (+100/175/275%) and eats Wild Boar. Both need Jade for their rank-2 fusion.
4. Dao marks: +5% strength per adopted own-branch policy; a cross-path Gu on a unit gives −10%; only path units reach
   ranks 2–4, so an unpathed clan stalls at Rank One with a Jade Skin Gu.
5. AI split two against two: Gu Yue and Bai lean Moon (they compete for orchids); Xiong and Wu lean Strength (they
   compete for pork). Everyone competes for jade.

**Later candidates:** Enslavement, Blood, Water/Ice (Bai Ning Bing), Earth, Lightning, Wind, Wood, Light (Little Light
Gu), Wisdom, Time (Spring Autumn Cicada), Theft, Poison, Fire.

## Pillar coverage (human and AI)

**P1 Power is expensive to keep.** Every unit of a major civ pays upkeep (`[-3] units cost no maintenance <for [Major]
Civilizations>` cancels the engine's three free units), except mortals: Clan Settlers and Mortal Laborers live off the
land (`[-100]% maintenance costs`, round 8). Rank multipliers (round 8): Rank One +50%; Moon +75/+100/+150%; Strength
+100/+125/+175%. Every Gu eats its canon food through `Consumes` on the promotion: Moonlight Gu → Moon Orchid; boar Gu →
Wild Boar (pork); Jade Skin Gu → Jade; White Jade Gu → 2 Jade (the fusion swaps pork for more jade, as in canon);
Moon Raiment → Moon Orchid; Liquor Worm → Wine. An unfed Gu goes through the [hunger meter](#hunger): weakened at
once, then a 50% death roll after 2 (rank 1) or 4 (rank 2) more turns of deficit, repeated every 3 or 5 turns. Stones come from rare Spirit Springs, so a clan's
upkeep capacity depends on the springs it holds. Further tiers: stones below 0 (−20% strength and Stone Shortage at
25% per turn), the engine's disband at −200. *AI:* the same formula times `aiUnitMaintenanceModifier`; every Gu grant
is gated on a food surplus, but the AI promotes all its units on one stale resource count [src], so it does overdraw;
the hunger meter then clears the deficit instead of leaving it forever. Ration (weight 2) is its way out of a stone
shortage.

**P2 Every breakthrough is a gamble.** Aptitude Awakening rolls a weighted die on every new Gu Master (A/B/C/D
5/20/35/40%). Refine Gu is the only promotion bought with XP, so all XP becomes gambles: careful, reckless, a Liquor
Worm lure, or cooling off. Fusions are harder than single rungs (70%/40% instead of 85%/50%); a spare wild boar is the
canon tusk catalyst (+20 points for White Jade Gu). The Flower Wine Monk Inheritance is a double-edged prize.
*AI:* buys Refine Gu like any promotion and resolves the Alert by weights; Refine Gu and the refining choices close in
every state where no rung can be gained (14,742 states checked), so the AI never pays for an empty attempt.

**P3 Heaven and earth are without benevolence.** Beast Tides hit every major at random (3% per turn after T30, +2%
after T90; random timing staggers civs, E6) and escalate by era up to each civ's own Thunder Crown Wolf. Lairs spawn
Lightning Wolves, and Bold Lightning Wolves once every civ knows Rank Three Cultivation [src]. Inheritances can trap.
Preparation (Stockade Wall, a stone reserve) opens better choices. *AI:* everything fires automatically.

**P4 Specialists beat dabblers.** Moon and Strength exclude each other; path units require their branch; unpathed
clans cap at Rank One + Jade Skin Gu; cross-path Gu cost −10%; dao marks reward depth. *AI:* personalities put one path
first (Gu Yue Moon 10, Bai Moon 8, Xiong Strength 10 and starts with it adopted, Wu Strength 10).

**P5 Righteous and demonic are strategies.** The branches exclude each other. Righteous: −25% upkeep on friendly land,
city-state favour, defence, clan law. Demonic: stones from every kill, plunder, the Lone Cultivators policy (a small
cheap elite core, the S6 rogue strategy), the strike-first and caravan-robbery choices; paid for with faster
city-state decay, unrest and a 3% Betrayal chance per turn. *AI:* Wu starts Righteous; Xiong leans
Demonic; personalities set the rest.

| Spirit Spring | 灵泉 | ADAPTED | A spring of heaven-and-earth energy that yields primeval stones; each Qing Mao clan built its village on one and guards it as its life.<br>*Note: Verified: primeval stones come from the spirit spring (灵泉, later 元泉) (ch15); Qing Mao has three, one per clan (ch180); the Gu Yue village was founded on its spring (ch4). ## World conflicts (canon overhaul, 2026-10-19)

The user's decision D4: **all four majors start righteous** (`Starts with [Righteous Path] adopted`), as every Qing Mao
clan and the Wu are in canon; Xiong's demonic lean is gone (it was an adaptation of its hired demonic Gu Masters,
R V1c175). The demonic camp is reached through events, and a camp can change both ways, as for the Fang clan and
Wei Yang in canon. The engine cannot force war, peace or another civ's opinion, so the conflicts work through marks,
bounties, pacts, temperaments and events (conflicts research, docs/CANON_OVERHAUL.md part C).

| Object | 中文 | Status | Lore basis | Mechanical role | Uniques |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Demonic Infamy | 魔名 | ADAPTED | The righteous hunt demonic cultivators and post bounties and wanted notices for them (R V2c29, V2c35). | Marker on every military unit of a Demonic Path clan (given on adoption and to new units); it clears itself once the clan's camp changes. Righteous clans get +10% against such units and, with Righteous Path Complete, a bounty of the unit's cost (C1).<br>*Fields:* no unitTypes | `Comment [Righteous clans fight harder against this unit and pay a bounty for it]`<br>`[This Unit] loses the [Demonic Infamy] promotion <upon turn start> <before adopting [Demonic Path]>` |

Righteous Alliance enables defensive pacts (C2, the written three-village alliance, R V1c127): only two righteous
clans can pact. Beast Tide gains choice 5 "Call the clans' alliance" (C3); Stone Shortage choice 4 is open to every
clan, shunned by the merchants, and exposes a righteous robber with a 50% chance (C7). The Flower Wine Monk
Inheritance already carried its grudge against Gu Yue (C6).

Triggers in GlobalUniques:

```
Triggers a [Collusion Exposed] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Righteous Path]> <when number of [Adopted [[Demonic Path] branch] Policies by [Friendly] Civilizations] is more than [0]> <with [4]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Quick] game speed>
Triggers a [Collusion Exposed] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Righteous Path]> <when number of [Adopted [[Demonic Path] branch] Policies by [Friendly] Civilizations] is more than [0]> <with [3]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Standard] game speed>
Triggers a [Collusion Exposed] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Righteous Path]> <when number of [Adopted [[Demonic Path] branch] Policies by [Friendly] Civilizations] is more than [0]> <with [2]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Epic] game speed>
Triggers a [Collusion Exposed] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Righteous Path]> <when number of [Adopted [[Demonic Path] branch] Policies by [Friendly] Civilizations] is more than [0]> <with [1]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Marathon] game speed>
Triggers a [Whitewashed Name] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Demonic Path]> <if [Shang Clan City] is constructed by anybody> <with [3]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Quick] game speed>
Triggers a [Whitewashed Name] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Demonic Path]> <if [Shang Clan City] is constructed by anybody> <with [2]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Standard] game speed>
Triggers a [Whitewashed Name] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Demonic Path]> <if [Shang Clan City] is constructed by anybody> <with [1]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Epic] game speed>
Triggers a [Whitewashed Name] event <upon turn start> <after adopting [Demonic Path]> <if [Shang Clan City] is constructed by anybody> <with [1]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Marathon] game speed>
Triggers a [Clan Contest] event <upon turn start> <after turn number [40]> <when not at war> <with [3]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Quick] game speed>
Triggers a [Clan Contest] event <upon turn start> <after turn number [60]> <when not at war> <with [2]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Standard] game speed>

Triggers a [Clan Contest] event <upon turn start> <after turn number [180]> <when not at war> <with [1]% chance> <for [Major] Civilizations> <on [Marathon] game speed>
```

## The Qing Mao Mountain map (D5, canon overhaul 2026-10-19)

`maps/Qing Mao Mountain` (40 × 26), built by `tools/make_map.py` with Unciv's own serializer (`tools/autoplay/MakeMap.java`).
The random generator places terrain by latitude and cannot put three clan villages on one mountain, so the mod ships
the canon setting (geography research):

| Place | Where | Canon |
|---|---|---|
| Qing Mao Mountain (青茅山) | centre: a ridge of peaks, hill slopes, spear-bamboo forest | R V1c7, V1c13 |
| Bai village (白家寨) | north, by the deep pool under the back-slope waterfall (a lake) | R V1c14, V1c65 |
| Gu Yue village (古月山寨) | halfway up, with moon orchids and the stone forest's jade | R V1c4, V1c79 |
| Xiong village (熊家寨) | south, on the front slope | R V1c65 |
| Spirit springs | one under each village, three contested on the slopes, two for Wu, one for Jia | R V2c183 (three or four on a famous mountain) |
| Lightning-wolf nest | Beast Lairs low on the mountain | R V1c31 |
| Yellow Dragon tributary, foothill villages | a river along the foot; the Foothill Villages city-state beside it | R V1c117, V1c124 |
| Jia village (贾家寨) | north-east, on its own mountain | R V1c45 |
| Hairy men | the western rainforest | R V2 |
| Rotting swamp | south-east marsh | R V2c8-9 |
| Wu Yi Mountain (武仪山) | east, by its river (ADAPTED: a Southern Border super clan drawn closer for a four-clan game) | R V5c300 |

Inheritance Sites (ruins) and wild resources are placed by hand; the starting locations allow "Select players from
starting locations". Autoplay on the map: 0 crashes, every clan alive at turn 250.

## Refinement ladder

**Cultivation (round 5d).** Every Gu Master standing in a city with a Gu Academy (or Wu's Martial Arena, which
replaces it) gains 2 XP per turn until it holds a Killer Move: the academy's seclusion pays for the next attempt.
Before, XP came only from battle, so the AI bought one attempt per unit and the ladder stalled at rung 1 (autoplay,
King, 200 turns: 0.7 rank-2 Gu and 0.04 Killer Moves per clan; with cultivation 5.1 and 2.8, and the stones and the
hunger meter both bite harder; docs/AUTOPLAY.md, round 5d).

Refine Gu opens the Refinement Attempt Alert on the unit. The careful and reckless choices carry the same ladder with
different odds. Each unit state matches **at most one** "gains" line, so one attempt rolls once; lines fire top to
bottom and promotion conditionals are read live [src], so one attempt climbs at most one rung. After a successful
fusion, deterministic "loses" lines remove the two ingredients.

| Unit | Rung 1 (85% / 50%) | Rung 2 — canon fusion (70% / 40%) | Rung 3 (85% / 50%) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Moon Master | Moonlight Gu (needs a Moon Orchid surplus) | Moon Raiment = Moonlight Gu + Jade Skin Gu (a spare Jade as material unless a Jade Skin is carried); a defensive Gu that helps nearby allies | Killer Move (Killer Moves tech) |
| Strength Master (without Moon Master) | White Boar Gu (needs a Wild Boar surplus) | White Jade Gu = White Boar Gu + Jade Skin Gu (1 spare Jade with a carried Jade Skin, 2 without); a spare Wild Boar is the tusk: 90% / 60% | Killer Move (Killer Moves tech) |
| Unpathed (neither Master), any rank | Jade Skin Gu (needs a Jade surplus): the cap | — | — |

**Off the ladder** (never named by a ladder line, so they add no state): Black Boar Gu (Jia ally, stacks as a second
boar), the clan emblems Bear Strength Gu and Stream Gu, Gu Yue's emblem Moonlight Gu on a Strength Master (only −10%),
and the Liquor Worm (Rank One only; it leaves at the next turn start after a breakthrough).

**Dead states.** The refining choices close when the unit has Killer Move; holds a rank-2 Gu before the Killer Moves
tech; or lacks the food its next rung needs (orchid, pork, or 1–2 jade). An unpathed Rank One is closed once it holds
Jade Skin Gu or when there is no Jade. Refine Gu itself closes in all of these states, except that a Rank One can
still buy an attempt when it can lure a Liquor Worm. It also closes below 50 HP; the reckless choice closes below
31 HP. The Unfinished Refinement ruin carries the same guard, so it is never drawn for a unit that cannot gain
anything. **Hunger:** a Gu whose food is in deficit is weakened and, after 2 (rank 1) or 4 (rank 2) more turns of
deficit, dies with a 50% chance per roll; a Killer Move whose rank-2 Gu died goes at the next turn start, and the unit can climb again.

**C3 (2026-10-19): the ladder keys on path promotions.** A rung belongs to the unit's path Master (`Moon Master`, or `Strength Master` without `Moon Master`, so a human unit holding both climbs the Moon ladder only); the rank-2 fusions need a unit above rank one; the nine non-Killer-Move gains close once a Killer Move is held. A Rank One on a path climbs rung 1 and stops there. tools/ladder_check.py models every path and rank combination (62,208 states, 0 errors). Refine Gu and the Unfinished Refinement ruin close on these 46 lines (each ladder dead state, crossed with the four ways a Liquor Worm lure is impossible):

## Events in detail

AI weight = product of the choice's `[relativeAmount]% weight to this choice for AI decisions` uniques (E1). The
percentages assume every choice is open; closed choices drop out and the rest are renormalised.

### Aptitude Awakening

Presentation **None**. Fired by every Gu Master unit's own unique when the unit is created (E3). Pure die: the odds are the weights.

**1. Grade A aptitude** — AI weight 0.05, 5% when every choice is open and no conditional weight applies; conditional weights: `[+100]% weight to this choice for AI decisions <if [Awakening Grounds] is constructed>`; `[+100]% weight to this choice for AI decisions <for [Bai Clan] Civilizations>`

```
[This Unit] gains the [Aptitude A] promotion
```

**2. Grade B aptitude** — AI weight 0.2, 20% when every choice is open and no conditional weight applies; conditional weights: `[+50]% weight to this choice for AI decisions <if [Awakening Grounds] is constructed>`; `[+25]% weight to this choice for AI decisions <for [Bai Clan] Civilizations>`

```
[This Unit] gains the [Aptitude B] promotion
```

**3. Grade C aptitude** — AI weight 0.35, 35% when every choice is open and no conditional weight applies

```
[This Unit] gains the [Aptitude C] promotion
```

**4. Grade D aptitude** — AI weight 0.4, 40% when every choice is open and no conditional weight applies; conditional weights: `[-25]% weight to this choice for AI decisions <if [Awakening Grounds] is constructed>`

```
[This Unit] gains the [Aptitude D] promotion
```

## Balance starting numbers

Starting points for Phase 2; every tunable number goes into `docs/BALANCE.md` once JSON exists, and the 100-turn
autoplay (MOD_SPEC §10, §11) decides.

**Unit upkeep in stones per turn**, from the engine formula [src `getUnitMaintenance`]: cost = (0.5 × Σ unit
weights × (1+p))^(1+p/3), p = turn / 500 on standard speed; weights multiply. Majors pay for every unit.

| Situation | Round 1 | Round 2 |
|---|---|---|
| T40, careful Moon player: 2 laborers, 3 Rank One, 1 Rank Two Moon with its rung-1 Gu | 4 | 4 |
| T40, careless: 2 laborers, 5 Rank Two Strength | 6 | 6 |
| T80, Xiong AI: 2 laborers, 2 Rank Three + 3 Rank Two Strength with their rung-2 Gu, 2 Rank One with an emblem Gu, 2 recruited wolves | 14 | 12 |
| T120, 2 laborers + 6 elite Rank Four Strength (rung-2 Gu + Killer Move) | 28 | 23 |
| T120, 2 laborers + 6 elite Rank Four Moon (rung-2 Gu + Killer Move) | 24 | 20 |

Round 2 lowers stone upkeep for rank-2 units because their Gu now eat jade instead of stones (the old White Jade Gu
cost +20% upkeep). The early P1 pressure therefore rests on the Gu foods and on building and road upkeep; whether that
is enough is the autoplay's question. **Event costs never push a non-negative treasury below zero:** every
stone-costing choice is gated by a `<when above [amount] [stat/resource]>` at or above its cost.

**Known limits, accepted for Slice 1 and watched in autoplay:**

1. Gaining or fusing a Gu does not refresh the civ's resource count [src], and the AI promotes all its units in one
   pass, so overdraws are normal AI play (also after a pillaged camp or mine, or a lapsed trade). The holders are
   weakened until new food closes the gap or the hunger meter's rolls kill enough Gu to close it.
2. Jade is the rank-2 bottleneck for both paths. Sources: hill deposits, the Refinement Chamber (+1 per city) and the
   Flower Wine Monk Inheritance (+2). The AI never trades for promotion food, so a jade-poor AI plateaus at rung 1
   (without wasting XP).
3. One luxury (Furs), and the map gives a regional luxury to one major only [src]: happiness is tight. Every
   difficulty gets +6 base happiness as the starting fix; the autoplay decides.
4. The AI cannot see that the Flower Wine Monk Inheritance destroys its own Awakening Grounds; Gu Yue's AI is told
   never to build it (it would also lose the flower sea's +2 orchids), the other AIs may still build it.
5. Population loss stops at 1 [src]: "Abandon the outer fields" is free for size-1 colonies. Single-city costs use the
   capital (`[-1] population [in capital]`) because a civ-level "random city" always picks the same city [src].
6. With barbarians disabled, "Meet the wolves" and "Hunt the traitor" summon nothing, so they cost nothing.
7. Ration can stack if Stone Shortage fires again during its 8 turns; the three Betrayal triggers can fire in the same
   turn; Culture has no sink once both allowed branches are complete; reloading a save rerolls chance rolls (E5); the
   victory screen shows "Max Turns" only for a victory named "Time" [src].
8. Spirit Springs are rare by design. Without the Strategic Balance option a clan may find no spring near its first
   cities; it then has no Awakening Grounds (and fewer stones) until it settles or takes one. That is the intended
   geopolitics, but the autoplay must show the AI coping with it.

## Engine facts this plan relies on

Each needs an in-game check in the Phase 2 step report (console commands in `docs/TESTING.md`).

| Fact | Source | Check in game |
|---|---|---|
| E1: a None event picks one choice by weighted random; AI civs resolve Alerts the same way | [src] `Event.kt` | create ~20 Gu Masters, count grades |
| E2: an event triggered from a promotion or ruin keeps the unit as context | [src] `getTriggerFunction` | Refine Gu grants the rung to that unit |
| E3: a unit's triggerable without trigger conditional fires once at creation, before starting promotions are added | [src] `UnitManager.placeUnitNearTile`, `BaseUnit.construct` | train Gu Yue Rank Ones with a Gu Academy: no D grade keeps a Moonlight Gu |
| E4: the AI never buys a "Doing so will consume…" promotion; a kept promotion with `<upon turn start>` self-removal is re-buyable at a rising XP price | [src] `NextTurnAutomation.applyPromotions`, `UnitPromotions` | `unit setxp`, buy Refine Gu, end turn, buy again; spectate an AI |
| Choice uniques fire in list order and read promotions live: one rung per attempt, and the fusion's "loses" lines run after the grant | [src] `Event.triggerChoice` | give a Strength unit White Boar + Jade Skin, refine: it ends with White Jade Gu only |
| `Remove [buildingFilter] [cityFilter]` fires when a building is constructed, and filters count a unique replacement (`replaces`) | [src] `CityConstructions`, `Building.matchesFilter` | build the Flower Wine Monk Inheritance as Gu Yue: the Moon Orchid Flower Sea disappears |
| City-state ally uniques reach newly trained units | [src] `Civilization.forEachMatchingUnique` | ally the Jia Clan, train a Gu Master: it has Black Boar Gu |
| Rebels ignore `Never appears as a Barbarian unit` and `Unbuildable`, spawn by the civ's first city, and are skipped without barbarians | [src] `UniqueTriggerActivation` | `civ activatetrigger "Triggers a [Beast Tide] event"` |
| Barbarians know only techs every civ knows (gates the Bold Lightning Wolf) | [src] `BarbarianManager` | spectate past Rank Three Cultivation |
| A consumed luxury still gives happiness once its net is negative, so Gu foods must be Strategic | [src] `CivInfoTransientCache` | — (design rule) |
| Hunger meter: turn-start triggers run before statuses tick down; a status that runs out fires `<upon losing the [promotion] status>`; reapplying a status never lowers its timer | [src] `UnitTurnManager.startTurn`, `MapUnit.setStatus`, `removeStatus` | trade away all Moon Orchid as Gu Yue: Moonlight Gu holders lose their bonus at once, get Orchid Hunger, and about half lose the Gu at the second turn start after that; survivors get Orchid Hunger again |
| `requiredNearbyImprovedResources` limits the Awakening Grounds to a city with an improved Spirit Spring; `Start bias` accepts a resource name | docs (Mod-file-structure 2, Unique-parameters `terrainFilter`) | start a game: each major's capital lies near a spring; the Awakening Grounds is offered only in a city whose spring has a Spring Cave |
| Ranged attackers never capture or recruit; the extra ranged attack before combat hits units only, never cities | [src] `BattleUnitCapture`, `Battle.tryExtraRangedAttack` | attack a wolf with a Moon unit: two damage numbers (blade, then melee); attack a city: one |

## How this plan was made

* **Round 1 (2026-10-12):** three independent drafts (lore, systems, player experience), a synthesis, and two skeptic
  reviews (lore, mechanics); their must-fixes and most should-fixes were applied (dead refinement states, double
  rolls, food gates, upkeep, population costs, CANON/ADAPTED tags). The Phase 0 claim E4 was corrected.
* **Round 2 (2026-10-13):** your answers were checked claim by claim against the raw novel text, each by a researcher
  and an independent skeptic. Two independent designs (lore fidelity, systems and AI) were then merged; the ladder was
  verified exhaustively and the whole plan by Unciv's own validator. A final lore skeptic and mechanics skeptic
  reviewed the result; their fixes are applied (starving Gu, the ruin guard, canon Moon Raiment, the Xiong survival,
  stone springs, happiness, AI weights).
* **Round 3 (2026-10-13):** your decisions: the Wu clan, the hunger meter (built on timed statuses after reading the
  engine source), Spirit Springs. Validated again with Unciv's own validator and the ladder check.
* **Round 4 (2026-10-13):** your option B for hunger deaths (a 50% roll per Gu, seeded per unit) and your approval:
  autoplay with Strategic Balance on decides the tuning.

## Roadmap after Slice 1

* **Slice 2 (Ascension):** rank 5, ascension through a kept, self-removing "Attempt Ascension" promotion (E4 route B),
  Blessed Lands, Heavenly Tribulations, immortal essence stones, desolate beasts, Spring Autumn Cicada, and Fang
  Yuan's own faction with S8 foreknowledge (decision 4).
* **Later recipes:** Tian Peng Gu (天蓬蛊, rank 3 after White Jade Gu), Black Mane Gu (黑鬃蛊 = Black Boar + Green Silk
  Gu), Moonglow Gu (月芒蛊 = Moonlight + two Little Light Gu), the Four Flavors Liquor Worm.
* **Later factions:** the Tie clan (law enforcers; its constable Tie Xue Leng reaches Qing Mao during the wolf tide,
  and its Demon Suppression Tower), the Shang clan as a major.
* **Later beasts and peoples:** Frenzy Lightning Wolf, Lightning Lycan, egg men and little people (Three Kings
  inheritance), stone men (a blessed land, volume 3).
* **Slice 3 (Five Regions):** the region-wall map, the other regions' factions, hostile terrain (S4), the Heavenly
  Court, the Eternal Life victory.

# Immortal arc — MOD_SPEC_2 Phase B (rank 5–9)

**Status: approved 2026-10-18 with the user's decisions B1–B5 below.** One row per rank 5–9 object within MOD_SPEC_2's
budget (as amended by MOD_SPEC_3: paths and killer moves live in `docs/PATHS.md`). No content JSON ships before
approval; Phase C builds it in layers C1–C5.

**Sources.** R = the raw Chinese text, cited as R V*v*/*n* (volume *v*, chapter *n* in that volume's numbering) or
R ch*N* (global chapter number, the fan wiki's numbering); the copies used are unofficial uploads and are never linked
from the mod. F = the Reverend Insanity fan wiki (page name), whose English names are used. CANON = attested in R
(F agrees unless noted); ADAPTED = canon bent to a 4X mechanic, or a canon detail that rests on F only; INVENTED =
mechanical glue. The research was done by subagents against R and F (MOD_SPEC_2 Phase B allows it); only verdicts are
kept here.

**Patterns, not instances.** The uniques below are written as documented patterns (`uniques.md` 4.22.8 placeholders);
the numbers come in Phase C and D. Every pattern was matched against the docs; the risky ones were proven in Phase A
(`docs/CAPABILITIES.md`, "MOD_SPEC_2 Phase A").

## Decisions (user, 2026-10-18)

* **B1. Eastern Sea major: the Zombie Alliance 僵盟** (headquartered there, it outranks the local super forces, R V4/4).
* **B2. Western Desert major: the Fang clan 房家** (a righteous super force, the world's best at Gu Houses; 房 is not
  Fang Yuan's 方). Correction 2026-10-19 (conflicts research): it is **not** the desert's first force; seizing Dou Shen
  Palace "would make it first" (R V5c499, V6c252), and three of its Gu Houses fighting together (V5/498) is not its
  total.
* **B3. All 15 inheritances stay**, three over MOD_SPEC_2's cap of 12, at the user's request (the cap was the only
  reason to cut Hu Immortal, Blood Sea Ancestor and the 88 True Yang Building).
* **B4. Canon:** dream realms open by era (rank 7 on), as they surface before the Fate War; a shattered Fate Gu sets
  off the qi tides and the fading of the region walls.
* **B5.** The seed corrections above are accepted.

## Stockpiles (10)

All `Stockpiled` resources. Essence **never regenerates by itself** (MOD_SPEC_2 #2): its only sources are blessed lands,
the grotto-heaven, one immortal Gu, trade, events and plunder.

| Object | Chinese | Verdict | Lore basis (source) | Sources and sinks (patterns) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Heaven Qi, Earth Qi, Human Qi | 天气 / 地气 / 人气 | CANON | The three qi of ascension; human qi is what the master accumulated in life, and more of it lets more heaven and earth qi fuse (R V5/346, V5/468) | Heaven and earth qi from land (`Instantly provides [positiveAmount] [stockpiledResource]` `<upon turn start>` `<for every [positiveAmount] [countable]>` on owned tiles); human qi from deeds (`Instantly gain [amount] [stockpile]` on victories and events); spent by the ascension (`<costs [amount] [stockpiledResource]>`) |

completion event may `Remove [buildingFilter] [cityFilter]`, reopening the race, or call down its own tribulation

## Inheritances (15, over the cap at the user's request, B3)

MOD_SPEC_2 #12, with the user's Phase A decision: **the trial unlocks building the inheritance** (a world wonder;
`Only available` `<if [buildingFilter] is constructed>` on a cheap seal the trial grants), so conquest can seize it.
Trials are thrown by the owned-tile trigger (`Triggers a [event] event` `<upon turn start>`
`<when number of [countable] is more than [countable]>` on Owned [tileFilter] Tiles, `<every [positiveAmount] turns>`;
`Unavailable` `<if [buildingFilter] is constructed by anybody>`, Phase A4), mix chance and preparation, and can cost units.
Giant Sun grades true inheritances ordinary / unparalleled / supreme (R V3/193–195).

## Victory

Eternal Life (MOD_SPEC_3): milestones `Build [building]` (Eternal Life) after Venerable Ascension; the Venerable races
its own lifespan. Domination (Slice 1) stays.

## Phase C1: ascension, essence, Rank 5–6 eras (built 2026-11-01)

MOD_SPEC_2 Phase C, first layer, from the rows above (Eras and techs, Stockpiles, Units, Buildings and wonders,
Events). What changed against the plan, and why:

* **Blessed lands are promotions on the immortal, not free buildings.** `Gain a free [buildingName] [cityFilter]`
  adds nothing to a city that already holds the building [src UniqueTriggerActivation GainFreeBuildings], so a
  second immortal of the same grade would get no land, and the building could not follow its owner. Canon puts the
  blessed land *inside* the immortal's aperture (R V4/290): as a promotion it belongs to the unit, every immortal has
  its own, the grade shows on the unit, and the land dies with it. C2's tribulations hit it on the unit (the user's
  decision: a timed status on the ascended unit). Verdict ADAPTED (mechanic), CANON (grades and their ratio 1:2:3:5).
* **Qi income is flat per turn; the ascension's cost scales with the game speed** (one line per speed, R7), so a
  Marathon game does not ascend three times as often per era.
* **The AI only tries the balanced ascension.** The promotion opens at the hasty amount for a human and at the
  balanced amount for the AI (<for [AI player] Civilizations>), so the AI never spends XP on an attempt it would
  rush (E4); its event weights still let it rush one time in sixteen.
* **Calamity Lore unlocks the ascension** (knowing the calamities is the last preparation, ADAPTED); C2 adds its
  preparation buildings. Dao Comprehension, the repeatable tech, moves to the end of the tree (column 10).
* **Rank-5 path Gu wait for the Rank Five Gu Master** (<for [post-[Rank Four era]] units>; they waited at rank four
  before). Rank-4 Gu go to any unit of rank four or higher (<for [post-[Rank Three era]] units>).
* **The careful-refinement and Beast Tide costs of the Rank Four era now hold in every later era**
  (<starting from the [Rank Four era]>), or they would fall back to free.

## Phase C2: calamities, tribulations, Heaven's Will, lifespan (built 2026-11-01)

MOD_SPEC_2 Phase C, second layer. The user's decision (2026-10-18): tribulation timing is a timed status on the
ascended unit, not `<every N turns>`. Each blessed-land promotion gives its immortal two countdown statuses; when one
runs out (E7) it throws the event from the unit, so the event can hurt that immortal and its land.

* **Earthly calamity** (地灾) every ten aperture-years (R V4/91): 14 / 13 / 12 / 11 turns for lower to super grade on
  Standard (a bigger land runs faster and meets them sooner, R V4/44); the first one 10–15 turns after the ascension
  (MOD_SPEC_2 tension target). **Heavenly tribulation** (天劫) every hundred aperture-years: 60 turns.
* **Prepared vs unprepared** (tension target: prepared under 10% of the land's output, unprepared 30% or more): the
  Formation Ward (10 damage) and the Stockpile Vault (40 stones) are the cheap ways; pouring essence costs 10 of the
  12–14 essence a lower-grade land makes per cycle; enduring it throws a silent die: the land lies ravaged for 8
  turns (50%: no essence and no stones, about 30% of a cycle's output on average), the immortal takes 40 damage (30%),
  or nothing (20%). A heavenly tribulation faced bare kills one immortal in four.
* **Lifespan Gu** (寿蛊) is a stockpile (Phase A5: it can go into debt). Forests, jungles and swamps yield a few (15% a
  turn per 8 owned tiles of each, after Calamity Lore; R V4/298: only heaven and earth make them, where life teems); a
  new immortal brings 5. Every immortal burns one each ten turns (one each five starved most immortals in autoplay); below one it cannot heal and takes 7 damage a turn, so
  it dies in about fifteen turns (tension target). The heaven path's Gu became the **Ten-Year Lifespan Gu** (十年寿蛊)
  so that the stockpile has the plain name.
* **Heaven's Will** (天意, R V5/54): a clan with more than two immortals draws a civ-level event, 3% a turn: lie low
  (-20% strength for 10 turns), pay 30 essence, or defy heaven (a Thunder Crown Wolf and two Bold Lightning Wolves rise
  in its land). Immortal Gu (C3) will join the trigger.
* Grand and myriad tribulations (ranks 7–8) will reuse the Heavenly Tribulation with era-scaled tolls in C5, which keeps
  the arc's events at the cap of 25 (the 20 planned plus C1's hasty die and result popups and C2's two toll dice).

| Object | Chinese | Verdict | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| Calamity Countdown, Tribulation Countdown | 地灾 / 天劫 | CANON (schedule), ADAPTED (turns) | Timers on the immortal |
| Ravaged Land | — | ADAPTED | Status: the land yields nothing |
| Earthly Calamity, Heavenly Tribulation | 地灾 / 天劫 | CANON | Popups from the unit |
| Calamity Toll, Tribulation Toll | — | INVENTED | Silent dice for the unprepared |
| Heaven's Will | 天意 | CANON | Civ-level popup |
| Formation Ward, Stockpile Vault | — | ADAPTED | National wonders (one per clan), Calamity Lore |
| Lifespan Gu | 寿蛊 | CANON | Stockpile |

## Phase C3: immortal Gu (rank six), dao marks, immortal moves (built 2026-11-01)

MOD_SPEC_2 Phase C, third layer, for the ranks the game has so far (rank six; ranks 7–9 come with their eras in C5).

* **Ten rank-six immortal Gu** (data/immortal_gu.csv), world wonders after **Immortal Gu Recipes** (Rank Six era,
  column 9): production plus 30–50 green grape essence (`Costs`), kept by a captured city (Phase A2: conquest seizes
  the Gu), never granted free (A3). Each carries the marker `Immortal Gu` and its path's marker (`Time Path`,
  `Strength Path`, …; allowlisted). Completion rolls the **Immortal Gu Refinement** die (仙蛊成劫, R V5/54): one in
  four births calls down a calamity on the city (2 population, two Bold Lightning Wolves). The plan's failed refinement
  that removes the Gu is dropped: an event cannot tell which wonder it was born from, and `Remove` on the marker would
  take every immortal Gu of the city.
* Effects follow the "Immortal Gu" table above, adapted where it pointed at later layers: Unravel Mystery gives +15%
  science until C4's inheritance trials; Divert Disaster adds a free "Divert the disaster" choice to every earthly
  calamity and heavenly tribulation; Spring Autumn Cicada and Connect Luck's rebirth lines live in GlobalUniques,
  gated on the wonder (a Gu Immortal reappears without a blessed land when one is lost: 50%, plus 25% with both).
* **Dao marks** (MOD_SPEC_2 #4, Phase A1): a path's Master gets +5% strength per immortal Gu of its own path its clan
  holds, and −5% per immortal Gu of a path it clashes with strongly (data/path_conflicts.csv at −25: earth–water,
  earth–fire, strength–water, metal–strength, light–dark). Lines exist only for paths that have immortal Gu.
* **Great Grandmaster** (大宗师), the third attainment tier of all 47 paths (docs/PATHS.md): rank six and up
  (post-[Rank Five era] units), +20% strength, and the path's **immortal killer move**, a one-turn status button
  that costs 5 green grape essence (MOD_SPEC_2 #5; human only, the AI never presses it). Three moves named after
  immortal Gu were renamed because the Gu are now wonders: Bone Spike Forest, Heart of Venom, Mystery Unravelled.
* **Heaven's Will** also strikes a clan holding more than two immortal Gu (MOD_SPEC_2 #10).
* Art: the ten wonders, the recipe tech, 47 Great Grandmaster icons and 47 immortal-move icons. The path promotions
  moved to their own atlas (`Images.Paths` → Paths.png).

| Object | Chinese | Verdict | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| Immortal Gu Recipes | 仙蛊方 | CANON | Tech: rank-six immortal Gu |
| Ten immortal Gu | see the table above | CANON (Divert Disaster ADAPTED) | World wonders |
| Immortal Gu Refinement | 仙蛊成劫 | CANON | Silent die on completion |
| `<Path> Great Grandmaster` (47) | 大宗师 | CANON (tier), ADAPTED (numbers) | Promotions |
| 47 immortal moves | docs/PATHS.md | see PATHS.md | One-turn statuses |

## Phase C4: inheritances, desolate beasts, the market (built 2026-11-01)

MOD_SPEC_2 Phase C, fourth layer, from the "Inheritances" and "Events" rows above.

* **Fourteen inheritances** (data/inheritances.csv) as world wonders, with the user's Phase A decision: a trial grants
  a **seal** (a free national building, `Unbuildable`; it need not be seizable, A3) that unlocks *building* the
  inheritance, so a rival holding the same seal races for it and conquest seizes it (A2). The trial is a silent die
  thrown by the owned-tile trigger (A4): a clan with more than 30 land tiles after Calamity Lore rolls it 2% a turn
  (Standard). Each inheritance is a rare outcome (weight ×0.1), four times likelier while the clan holds a Master of
  its path (preparation); a trap kills the seekers (2 population of the capital) about one roll in fifteen. Blood Sea
  Ancestor's pieces go only to a Demonic Path clan. Each prize is its path's +15–25% strength plus one civ-wide gift.
* **Left for later:** the 88 True Yang Building (only the Huang Jin may pass its trial; that nation comes with C5), and
  the region bindings (Central Continent, Northern Plains…): the game has no five-region map yet, so every
  inheritance can surface anywhere (ADAPTED).
* **Desolate Beast** (荒兽, about rank six, R V5/14): a barbarian elite (`Unbuildable`) that only events bring: the
  **Desolate Beast Tide** (2% a turn after Three Qi: hunt it, turn it aside with the Formation Ward for 80 stones, or
  let it rampage, −30% production for 5 turns) and Heaven's Will's "Defy heaven". Its corpse gives **Immortal
  Materials** (10% of its cost, 20).
* **Treasure Yellow Heaven** (宝黄天, MOD_SPEC_2 #16): 4% a turn for a clan with an immortal: buy 20 essence for 150
  stones, 3 Lifespan Gu for 120 (the AI's choice when it is short), sell 20 materials for 25 essence, sell 40 essence
  for 200 stones, or leave.
* **Secret realms (12 natural wonders) are not built yet.** They need the five-region map (T3) and tile art; random
  maps place only one to five natural wonders [MOD_SPEC_2 #13].

## Phase C5a: ranks seven and eight, grotto-heavens, rank 7–8 immortal Gu (built 2026-11-01)

MOD_SPEC_2 Phase C, fifth layer, first half. The other half (C5b: the arc's nations, Fate Gu and the Fate War, Gu
Houses, the Venerable and Eternal Life) follows.

* **Eras Rank Seven** (red date, R V4/141) and **Rank Eight** (white lychee, R V5/613); techs Red Date Essence, Rank
  Seven and Rank Eight Immortal Gu Recipes, Grotto-Heaven; Dao Comprehension moves to column 12.
* **Climbing a rank** (R V4/177: three hundred aperture-years of calamities lie between rank six and seven): every
  survived heavenly tribulation marks the immortal **Tribulation Survivor**; a kept promotion, **Advance to Rank
  Seven** (or Eight), opens for a survivor at home after its tech, pays 60 essence of its current rank (scaled per
  speed), drops the mark and upgrades the unit through the special upgrade (C1's route, with C3's crash guard). So each
  rank waits for one more tribulation. The one-turn **Advancing** status keeps the upgrade from counting as a lost
  immortal for Spring Autumn Cicada (E9: losing a unit also fires on upgrade).
* **Rank Seven Gu Immortal** (strength 85) keeps its blessed land, which now makes **red date essence**; **Rank Eight
  Gu Immortal** (115) trades the blessed land for a **Grotto-Heaven Aperture** (3 white lychee essence and 12 stones a
  turn; calamities every 10 turns, tribulations every 50). Both burn Lifespan Gu like rank six.
* **Seventeen rank-seven and rank-eight immortal Gu** (data/immortal_gu.csv), bought with red date or white lychee
  essence, with the table's effects; Divination Tortoise Shell hides its clan from Heaven's Will, Calamity Beckoning
  adds a profitable calamity choice, Treasure Light Gu a better market bargain. Every immortal-Gu effect that named
  rank six now covers every immortal (post-[Rank Five era] units).
* **Supreme Grandmaster** (无上大宗师), tier four of all 47 paths: rank eight, +25% strength; the space path's also
  passes through impassable tiles (a permanent promotion, never a status).
* **Ancient** (strength 85) and **Immemorial Desolate Beasts** (115) replace the desolate beast in the tides of the
  Rank Seven and Rank Eight eras; their corpses give 30 and 50 immortal materials.
* Art: both immortals and both beasts as 3D sprites with portraits and attack effects, 17 immortal Gu, four techs,
  two essences, five promotions, 47 Supreme Grandmaster icons; the immortal tiers moved to their own atlas (Arc.png).

## Phase C5b: the Venerable, Eternal Life, true inheritances, Gu Houses (built 2026-11-01)

MOD_SPEC_2 Phase C, fifth layer, second half; the arc's victory (MOD_SPEC_3: Eternal Life after Venerable Ascension).

* **Dao Lord** (column 12) opens the **Venerable Ascension**, a world wonder (one in the world at a time) that only a
  clan holding a rank-eight immortal of supreme attainment can build: every Supreme Grandmaster carries the marker
  `Supreme Attainment` (allowlisted) and the wonder counts a count of rank-eight immortals with that marker
  (the plan's single-path `max()` countable, simplified to "any one path at its summit"). It costs white lychee essence
  and brings forth the **Venerable** (strength 200): unbeatable in its era, but long-lived, never eternal (R V6/105;
  since Phase D its lifespan is the Lifespan Gu stock, not HP), and it makes 6 **yellow apricot essence** a turn.
* **Eternal Life** (world wonder; victory type **Eternal Life**, milestone `Build [Eternal Life]`): only a clan with a
  living Venerable can build it, and it costs 150 yellow apricot essence (300 since Phase D, 2026-11-02), about 25 of the Venerable's 33 turns: the
  Venerable races its own lifespan (MOD_SPEC_3).
* When a Venerable dies (losing one is its death: it never upgrades, E9), the Venerable Ascension is removed (A2: the
  race reopens) and its clan gains the **True Inheritance Seal**, which unlocks building the **True Inheritance**
  (+25% battle XP, immortals +20%), seizable by conquest. The **Venerable Falls** popup tells the clan.
* **Gu House Formation** (column 12) opens three **Gu Houses** (R V4/356), a new unit type: the Calamity Luck Altar
  (needs Connect Luck), the Myriad Year Flying Warship (Years Flow Like Water) and the Dragon Palace (Dream Token); one
  each per clan, white lychee essence on top of production, +500% upkeep. The Heaven Overseeing Tower waits for Fate
  Gu and the Heavenly Court.
* **Still to come** (the arc's nations and world state): the Heavenly Court and Fate Gu, the Fate War and the qi
  tides, the Huang Jin, the Zombie Alliance and the Fang clan, the immortal zombie, the four city-states, the rank-nine
  immortal Gu, the 88 True Yang Building, the secret realms and the five-region map.
* Art: the Venerable (three halos and a sun disc), the three Gu Houses in 3D, nine icons.
* Crash guard (autoplay): Unciv places an upgraded unit before copying its promotions, so a Water Master that
  ascended on open water found no tile and the game crashed restoring it. Ascension and advancement carry
  `Unavailable <in [Water] tiles>`, and Aperture Awakening (the starting tech) now has
  `Enables embarkation for land units`, so any upgrade at sea can place the new unit (ADAPTED: Gu Masters cross water
  by boat from the start).

## Phase C5c: the arc's nations, Fate Gu, the Fate War (built 2026-11-01)

MOD_SPEC_2 Phase C, last layer, from the "Nations", "Immortal Gu" and "Events" rows above.

* **Four arc majors** (B1, B2), all starting righteous (D4): the **Heavenly Court** (Duke Long; +15% against immortal
  zombies; the only builder of Fate Gu and of the Heaven Overseeing Tower), the **Huang Jin Family** (+15% defending;
  the only clan that may pass the 88 True Yang Building's trial), the **Zombie Alliance** (a fallen immortal rises
  as an **Immortal Zombie**; its immortals never age: one Lifespan Gu per immortal every ten turns offsets their
  burn) and the **Fang Clan** (Gu Houses +50% production, +10% science). Leaders the novel never names carry their
  title (Huang Jin Patriarch, Zombie Alliance Elder, Fang Clan Head). Voices in the mod's own words.
* **Four city-states:** Lang Ya Sect (Refiner Tribe), Spirit Affinity House and Combat Immortal Sect (a new type,
  Immortal Sect: friends get `[+2 Culture] [in capital]`, allies also `[+15]% Strength <for [post-[Rank Five era]] units>`),
  Merman Imperial Court (Merchant Clan).
* Personalities: the Court wants Fate Gu (`[+500]% weight to [Fate Gu] for AI decisions`), the Fang clan its Gu Houses
  (`[+200]% weight to [Gu House Formation] for AI decisions`).
* **Fate Gu** (rank nine, `uniqueTo` the Heavenly Court, after Immortal Gu Recipes; ADAPTED: the Court refines it
  during the arc instead of holding it from the start): immortals +15%, +2 happiness; immortal zombies −15% while it
  stands anywhere. A non-Court clan that conquers its city fights the **Fate War**: **shatter** it (removed for good:
  the clan gains the marker Shattered Fate, Fate Gu becomes unbuildable, +20% strength for 20 turns) or **seize** it
  (marker Fate Seized, no second popup). While Fate Gu lies shattered the **Qi Tides** sweep every clan (3% a turn):
  shelter the cities (−25% production) or ride the tide (qi, −20% strength). B4: dream realms do not wait for it.
* **Rank-nine immortal Gu:** Wisdom Gu and Derivation Gu after Rank Nine Immortal Gu Recipes, bought with yellow
  apricot essence (only a Venerable makes it). **88 True Yang Building** joins the inheritances (Huang Jin only).
* **Still not built:** the twelve secret realms and the five-region map (T3), dream realms, Treasure Yellow Heaven's
  essence stones, the Shadow Sect events.

## Rank Nine era: the Venerables (the user, 2026-11-01)

The user asked for rank nine as an era, "very dominating, very frightening, very terrible", overriding MOD_SPEC_2's
"Rank 9 is the victory, never an era". Canon: a Venerable is unbeatable in its era (R V6/105), its will spans
regions, Heaven's Will thins the Lifespan Gu of the world while one lives (R V5/54), and a chaos calamity from
outside the world is the rank-nine trial (R V6/280).

* **Rank Nine era** (yellow apricot), techs Rank Nine Immortal Gu Recipes and **Venerable Dominion** (column 13),
  Dao Comprehension at column 14.
* **The Venerable** (strength 280, 5 moves): three strikes a turn, moves after attacking, ignores terrain and walks
  through impassable tiles, enslaves the defeated, every enemy military unit beside it fights 25% weaker, +50%
  against cities, burns improvements; a human-only killer move doubles its strength for a turn (20 yellow apricot).
  Its life (Phase D, the user: "kenapa venerable darahnya setiap langkah berkurang terus?"): it no longer loses 3 HP a
  turn; like every immortal it burns a Lifespan Gu each ten turns on Standard and, with none left, takes 7 damage a
  turn and heals only by pillaging. HP is battle damage only; each kill heals 10 HP.
* **Chaos calamity** every 25 turns on the Venerable: 40 yellow apricot essence, or 25 HP.
* **Phase D (autoplay):** the AI never queued the Venerable Ascension or Eternal Life wonders, even with every condition
  met (a headless probe showed the wonder available, only the essence missing; one AI clan sat on 625 white lychee
  essence for 110 turns). Both became promotions the AI buys (E4): **Ascend to Venerable** (a rank-eight immortal of
  supreme attainment, at home, 60 white lychee) upgrades the immortal itself into the Venerable (canon: the immortal
  ascends) and grants the Venerable Ascension as the one-in-the-world marker; **Seek Eternal Life** (the Venerable,
  150 yellow apricot, 300 since Phase D) grants the Eternal Life wonder, which wins. Both wonders are now unbuildable markers. Also:
  heavenly tribulations every 30 turns at rank seven (canon: every 50 years against 100 at rank six), immortals
  cultivate 3 XP a turn (rank eight 8, the Venerable 5), essence pouring weighted down for the AI. Autoplay 8 × 550:
  an AI Bai Clan became a Venerable at T342 and won Eternal Life at T373.
* **The world's dread** while a Venerable lives: every other clan −10% strength and −2 happiness, the **Venerable's
  Shadow** event (4% a turn: tribute, hide the clan's Gu, or defy and lose population), every clan loses a Lifespan
  Gu each ten turns; the Venerable's own clan +20% production and +3 happiness.

## Dao marks: (the user, 2026-11-01: "dao marknya mana?")

Until now dao marks (道痕) were only a hidden line on each path's Master (MOD_SPEC_2 #4: +5% strength per immortal Gu
of its own path, −5% per immortal Gu of a strongly clashing path). They stay, and dao marks become visible on the unit:

* **Dao Marks I–V**, five promotion tiers that replace one another: +5%, +10%, +15%, +20% (heal +10), +30% (heal +15).
  Unciv draws I–III as stars on one icon; IV and V have their own.
* **Growth:** a path's Master of rank three or more holds the **Dao Comprehension** status (20 turns on Standard);
  when it runs out the marks grow one tier (E7). An immortal also takes a tier from every immortal it kills (absorbing
  its marks) and from every heavenly tribulation it survives. Each trigger climbs one tier only (highest tier checked
  first, like the refinement ladder). Mortals hold at most tier II (canon: a mortal's dao marks are few; an immortal's
  run to thousands).
* **Gates:** Advance to Rank Seven needs tier II, Advance to Rank Eight tier III, Ascend to Venerable tier IV (canon:
  rank nine needs some hundred thousand main-path marks, R V6/119).
* Autoplay 8 × 520: at turn 300, 363 Gu Masters at tier II, 17 / 11 / 10 immortals at tiers III / IV / V; rank seven
  reached by 26 of 32 clans, rank eight by 3, one AI Venerable (T497); 0 crashes.

Exact uniques (written by tools/lore_blocks.py from jsons/; every non-legacy Gu Master unit carries the Rank Six

## The Five Regions map: (T3; built 2026-11-01)

`maps/Five Regions` (60 × 40, built by `tools/make_map.py` through Unciv's own serializer, reproducible): the Central
Continent in the middle, the Northern Plains, the Western Desert, the Eastern Sea and the Southern Border around it.

* **Region walls** (界壁, R V5/22, V4/61, V4/279) as five impassable land terrains between the regions: Miasma (south),
  Licorice (north), Azure Water (east), Raging Flame (west), Sage (centre). Mortals cannot cross; every immortal unit
  (ranks six to eight, the Venerable, the immortal zombie, the Gu Houses) carries "Can pass through impassable tiles"
  (permanent: it is cached, never on a status) and takes 20–30 damage if it stops inside. So each region plays its
  own mortal game and the regions meet when their immortals do (canon: only immortals cross the walls). The walls do
  not fade after the Fate War (no unique changes terrain); the qi tides stand for it.
* **Starts in canon regions:** Gu Yue, Bai, Xiong and Wu with the Hairy Men, the foothill villages and the Jia clan on
  the Southern Border; Huang Jin on the Northern Plains; the Fang clan on a desert oasis (autoplay: on bare desert its
  settler never founded a city); the Zombie Alliance and the merfolk on Eastern Sea islands; the Heavenly Court and
  three sects in the centre.
* **Secret realms in their regions:** Hu Immortal Blessed Land, Dang Hun Mountain, Tian Ti Mountain, Luo Po Valley,
  Door of Life and Death (Southern Border, volume 2); Imperial Court Blessed Land, Crazed Demon Cave, Book Mountain,
  Reverse Flow River (Northern Plains, volume 3); Dragon Whale Paradise (Eastern Sea); Lang Ya Blessed Land and the
  River of Time in the centre (ADAPTED: one floats, the other lies outside the regions).
* Autoplay 6 × 400 with all eight majors: 0 crashes, every arc major alive, the Southern Border clans fight each other.
* **Each region's riches** (Phase D): beyond the start rings, spirit springs, jade, wild boar, moon orchids, furs,
  buried inheritances and beast lairs scattered at Qing Mao Mountain's density (a spring per ~100 land tiles; 28
  springs in all), and the Yellow Dragon River north of the Southern Border villages. Autoplay before: one spring per
  clan, stones per turn 3.0 at T80 and 42 of 48 clans 20+ turns in the red by T400; after: 6.1 at T80, 32 of 48.
  The rest of the late debt is the engine's AI, which builds soldiers while its stones per turn are positive and up
  to five per city, then pays the higher upkeep of every rank they climb (KNOWN_ISSUES).

## Secret realms, dream realms, the Shadow Sect: (built 2026-11-01)

* **Twelve secret realms** (data/secret_realms.csv; the "Secret realms and landmarks" rows above) as natural wonders.
  Without the five-region map they appear on random maps: ModOptions raise the count (`naturalWonderCountMultiplier`
  0.3, `naturalWonderCountAddedConstant` 1: about five on a Small map, twelve on a Huge one). Each yields stats without
  a worker and grants its blessing for the rest of the game to land military units beside it (+XP, +strength,
  +defence, healing, sight or movement); Luo Po Valley and Crazed Demon Cave can be entered and hurt units that end
  their turn inside (15 and 20). Tiles drawn for the default tileset (HexaRealm, 64x56) and its fallback (FantasyHex).
  The five-region map and its region walls (T3) are still not built.
* **Dream realms** (B4: they surface before the Fate War, from the Rank Seven era on): a civ-level event, not the
  camp the plan proposed (Phase A7's camp test showed spawning follows the camp marker, and a dream camp would only
  breed beasts): enter the dream (+50% battle XP and −10% strength for 10 turns) or let it fade.
* **Shadow Sect Envoy** (R V4/367): essence for a secret favour; a righteous clan that accepts may be exposed
  (Collusion Exposed), or the clan hunts the envoy.

## Phase D: power scaling (the user, 2026-11-02: "powerscalling sangat jelas dan detail")

The request: each path's attacks distinct and clear, its Gu attacks distinct within and across paths, the quantity of
dao and the comprehension of the path each with a clear, different effect. Before this, the four attainment tiers
gave only +10/+15/+20/+25% Strength, killer moves were one of seven family templates, and dao marks were a flat bonus.

* **Hit (Master):** every path has its own attack effect from `data/path_powers.csv` (extra damage, a status on the
  target, movement taken, healing, XP, stones), firing when the unit attacks. Twelve new target statuses (Burning,
  Frozen, Bleeding, Blinded, Soul-Torn, Dazed, Guard Broken, Entangled, Shocked, Doomed, Aged, Weighted) join the five
  of C3, each with a designed icon (the path's symbol over a falling wedge). Verdict ADAPTED: each effect follows the
  path's character in the novel (fire burns, ice freezes, blood drinks, theft steals), the numbers are the mod's.
* **Quantity of dao:** each dao-mark tier raises the hit (extra damage 4/5/6/8/10/12, status turns 2/2/2/3/3/4,
  healing 5..15, XP 2..8, stolen stones 10..40%, movement 1..2; some paths multiply them). The Master's promotion
  carries one line per tier, so the promotion screen shows what each tier adds.
* **Comprehension of the path:** Grandmaster opens a second effect, Great Grandmaster a domain or new art, Supreme
  Grandmaster the path's peak (most often its signature raised). Killer moves: the family's burst plus the path's hit
  at full power (doubled for the immortal move).
* **Clarity:** every civilopedia line of a tier or move, the PATHS.md "Power scaling" table and nine Tutorial pages
  (Civilopedia > Tutorials, "Gu World: ...") are written from the uniques by `tools/path_powers.py`; the game's own
  notifications name each effect when it lands ("... due to being damaged by a ...").
* **Not possible:** a different attack animation per path. Unciv chooses the animation by unit name or type, never by
  promotion (KNOWN_ISSUES 9).
* Autoplay (Five Regions, 6 × 350, seed 401): with hits on defence as well, besiegers took every defender's hit while
  cities take none: captured cities 16 → 9, clans destroyed 7 → 1. Attack-only hits: 27 and 9, 0 crashes.
* **The Venerable** no longer loses 3 HP a turn (the user: "kenapa venerable darahnya setiap langkah berkurang
  terus?"): lifespan is its Lifespan Gu stock, like every immortal's; HP is battle damage only.

## Phase D: immortal lifespan grows with rank; Gu hunger in percent (the user, 2026-11-02)

The user: "kalo udah ascend ke abadi umurnya bertambah bukan malah berkurang ... kasih tutor bagaimana cara menambah
umur gu abadi rank 6, 7, 8 dan 9 ... naik ke gu rank 9 susah dan prosesnya lama", and "kasih rate persentase seberapa
lapar dan apa makanan gu tersebut".

* **Lifespan** (`tools/lifespan.py`; ADAPTED: canon gives immortals far longer lives than mortals and lets Lifespan Gu
  extend them, R V3/194; the numbers are the mod's). The clan's Lifespan Gu stock is its immortals' lifespan; each
  rank reached adds to it (ascension 20, rank seven 25, rank eight 35, the Venerable 60) and a higher rank burns it more
  slowly (one each 10, 15, 20, 30 turns on Standard), so a rank's own gift lasts about 200, 375, 700 and 1800 turns.
  Before: the ascension gave 5 and every rank burned one each 10 turns, about 50 turns of life. Starving (7 damage a
  turn, healing only by pillage) is unchanged. A Tutorial page, "Gu World: Immortal Lifespan", lists every way to add
  lifespan (climbing, green land, Year Gu, Years Flow Like Water, Red Lotus, the market, conquest) and what takes it.
* **Immortals against mortals:** the user accepts that a clan with immortals outclasses mortal clans, because the climb
  is long and hard. To keep rank nine hard and long once immortals stopped dying of age, Eternal Life costs 300 yellow
  apricot essence instead of 150 (autoplay: a Venerable in 6 of 6 games, T332–T394; the win 59–66 turns later).
* **Hunger meter in percent** (`tools/gu_rules.py`): each Gu that eats a Gu food shows its own meter, "<Gu> Hunger
  <p>%", one step per turn start the clan lacks its food (rank one 33/67/100, rank two 20..100, rank three 14..100,
  rank four 11..100); the death roll comes at 100%, exactly when the R3 timer rolled it, and the meter starts again.
  Icons: the food over a bowl inside a ring filled to the percent. Every Gu promotion names its food in a visible
  comment (a Gu food and its hunger rate, or primeval stones and the upkeep it adds); the Tutorial page "Gu World: Gu
  Hunger and Food" lists every Gu. The four per-food statuses of R3 stay defined, unused, for old saves.

## Phase D: regional conflict (the user, 2026-11-02)

The user: "konflik di barat, utara dan timur laut tidak ada ... buat konflik di 4 daerah tersebut berperang antar klan
ataupun dengan gu master penyendiri maupun gu master iblis"; decided: minor clans (city-states) and wild factions.

* **Lone Gu Masters** (散修) and **demonic Gu Masters** (魔道蛊师) are canon everywhere in the novel: rogue cultivators
  without a clan and demonic-path cultivators who rob and kill for Gu (ADAPTED as barbarian units). Lone Gu Master
  (strength 14, rank two), Demonic Gu Master (22, rank three), Demonic Elder (44, rank five); camps spawn them once
  every clan knows the tech [src BarbarianManager]. The **Demonic Den** is a second camp kind: new camps pick a Beast
  Lair or a Demonic Den at random [src createNewCamp], and the Five Regions map places dens in the regions that had a
  single clan (north 2, west 2, east 1, centre 2).
* **Demonic Raid** (from T60 on Standard, 2% a turn per clan): fight (two Demonic Gu Masters, or an elder and two
  after Purple Crystal Essence, spawn near the capital as barbarians) or pay 80 stones. **Wandering Gu Master** (from
  T30, 2%): hire him for 60 stones (a free Rank Two Gu Master), send him away, or rob him (40 stones, he turns rogue).
* **Minor clans** (city-states), every name found in the raw Chinese text (R) and on the fan wiki (W) by the faction
  research of 2026-11-02:

  | Region | City-state | Chinese | Source | Type | Verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Northern Plains | Man Tribe (Red Flame Valley) | 蛮家 | R ch442-443; W | Battle Tribe, Hostile: swallows small tribes | CANON |
  | Northern Plains | Ge Tribe | 葛家 | R ch426, 440, 455; W | Mortal Villages, Friendly: poor, bullied by the Man | CANON (camp name INVENTED) |
  | Northern Plains | Lang Ya Sect (moved from the centre) | 琅琊派 | R ch461, 464 ("外界北原才一天"); W | Refiner Tribe | CANON |
  | Western Desert | Lan Clan (Zhou Xi Oasis) | 兰家 | R ch694; W | Refiner Tribe | CANON |
  | Western Desert | Mo Clan (Sand Wolf City) | 莫家 / 沙狼城 | R ch675, 1973; W | Battle Tribe | CANON |
  | Western Desert | Xiao Clan (Silk Corridor) | 萧家 / 万里丝廊 | R ch837, 908; W | Merchant Clan: richest traders | CANON (city ADAPTED from their Gu House) |
  | Eastern Sea | Song Clan (Heavy Water Blessed Land) | 宋家 / 重水福地 | R ch893, 899, 2102; W | Immortal Sect | CANON |
  | Eastern Sea | Xia Clan | 夏家 | R ch1981-1985; W | Battle Tribe: weakest, borders the Merman court | CANON (harbour name INVENTED) |
  | Central Continent | Immortal Crane Sect (Fei He Mountain) | 仙鹤门 | R ch193; W | Immortal Sect: hunts demonic Gu Masters | CANON |

  **Battle Tribe** is a fifth city-state type: its friends get a Gu Master every ~20 turns
  (`Provides military units every ≈[20] turns`), its ally every ~17 (`Provides military units every ≈[17] turns`),
  Vanilla's Militaristic numbers, so the region's majors are armed by their minor clans. Unciv has no unique that makes a civ
  declare war; wars come from the AI majors, the alliances the clans pull them into, the dens and the raids.
  Lang Ya was placed in the centre in C5c; the raw text puts it in the Northern Plains, so the sect and its blessed
  land moved north (a correction of my own mistake, not a user decision).
* **Why the arc majors still never fight each other** (autoplay, docs/AUTOPLAY.md "Phase D"): the region walls keep
  them apart, and the AI declares war only with happiness above 0, at least 4 soldiers and one per city, and 12
  population [src DiplomacyAutomation.declareWar]; the arc majors miss one of these most turns. So the regions' fights
  come from three canon sources instead:
  * the arc majors' temperaments turn warlike (Huang Jin aggressive 8 and declareWar 8: the plains tribes swallow
    small tribes, R ch443; Fang 6/7: the desert's clans gang up on rivals, R ch1775; Zombie Alliance 7/7; the Court
    5/5), so they attack the minor clans beside them;
  * demonic Gu Masters prey on every minor clan (1% a turn from T40), four times as often on the Eastern Sea
    (Song, Xia, the Merman court, and the Zombie Alliance itself; R ch1650: the sea's lone and demonic cultivators are
    the most numerous and the strongest), and the sea has two dens;
  * the Heavenly Court's Demon Extermination List (诛魔榜, R ch700, ch710) makes it 25% stronger against barbarians.

## Phase D: Gu catalog (the user, 2026-11-02)

The user: "Gu yang cuma sampe rank 4 dan terbatas ... minimal buat gu itu sampe Rank 9" and "buat gu tambahan sekitaran
100 lagi"; decided: canon first, the rest adapted. 113 Gu (`data/gu_catalog.csv`, `tools/gu_catalog.py`):
11 / 15 / 18 / 17 / 15 at ranks 1-5, 14 / 10 / 9 / 4 immortal Gu at ranks 6-9, every one of the 47 paths covered.

* **Sources.** 104 names were found in the raw text: 100 CANON (five with the rank from the wiki) and four with an
  adapted rank because the text gives none (Whip, Iron Rod, Gentle Feelings, Play Phantom). Kill is [VERIFY rank]. Four
  are INVENTED because the novel names no Gu for those paths: Mirage Veil (illusion), Furnace Pill (pill), Array Stone
  (formation) and Ink Beast (painting). The table with every source is in docs/PATHS.md "Gu catalog"; the research
  verdicts, the wiki errors it found and the canon killer moves are in docs/GU_RESEARCH.md.
* **How a unit gets one.** It spends XP on the Gu, as on any promotion. It must first hold the path's Master, and its
  rank must reach the Gu's: a rank-7 immortal Gu waits for a rank-seven immortal. The AI buys them as well: in 2
  autoplay games of 250 turns it held 54 different catalog Gu, 213 copies in all, immortal Gu included.
* **What it does.** Each Gu's effect comes from its role (strength, attack, defence, extra damage, a status, movement,
  healing, sight, stealth, an aura on enemies, XP, upkeep, plunder) and grows with its rank.
* **What it eats.** Mortal Gu eat stones (2-6% more upkeep). Moonglow Gu eats Moon Orchid and the Seven Fragrances
  Liquor Worm eats Wine, through the hunger meter. Immortal Gu eat their rank's immortal essence and sleep while the
  clan has none.
* **Immortal Gu (ADAPTED).** Canon makes every immortal Gu one of a kind. The unit-held immortal Gu break that rule on
  purpose, as the user decided; the one-of-a-kind canon immortal Gu stay wonders.
* **Art and sound.** Each Gu has its own designed icon (`tools/art/guart.py`): its form (37 kinds: beetle, moth,
  centipede, gong, web...), its path's badge, and its rank shown as diamonds, or a halo with stars for immortals. Each
  also has its own refinement sound (`tools/art/sfx.py` `gu_awaken`): its voice (wings, clicks, chime, metal or flow),
  then its path's strike at its own pitch, with a dao bell for immortals.
